{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgRALEJU9sG2AqAQoVLQX","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-AmountValidation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Amount Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Parse input amounts into signed integer cents, rejecting zero and malformed values. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.validation.amount_cents |\n| Consumes | cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command that accepts a money amount (`txn post`, `txn amend`, `transfer`, `load transactions`) converts the text to integer cents through one function. Money is never a float at any layer.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.validation.amount_cents(text: str) -> int`\n\n- Returns signed integer cents, a plain `int`.\n- Raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) for any value that is not accepted.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The text must match `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]` over the whole string: an optional minus, one or more ASCII digits, then optionally a period followed by one or two ASCII digits. Use a full match, so a trailing newline is not tolerated.\n2. Conversion uses string splitting and integer arithmetic only. A one-digit fraction is tens of cents (`5.5` is 550 cents); no fraction means whole units.\n3. A value equal to zero (`0`, `0.0`, `0.00`, `-0`, `-0.00`) is rejected.\n4. Rejected shapes include three or more decimals (even `1.000`), a leading `+`, an empty string, surrounding whitespace, a bare `.`, a missing integer part (`.5`), a trailing period (`1.`), thousands separators, exponents, and non-numeric text.\n5. No `float`, `Decimal` of a float, or `round` appears in the implementation.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC amount-decimal-forms ===\nIntent: Two-decimal, one-decimal, whole, and negative inputs convert to the integer cents derived from the supplied parts.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\n\nwhole = 12\nfrac = 50\nassert amount_cents(f\"{whole}.{frac:02d}\") == whole * 100 + frac\n\ntenths = 5\nassert amount_cents(f\"{whole}.{tenths}\") == whole * 100 + tenths * 10\n\nassert amount_cents(str(whole)) == whole * 100\n\nassert amount_cents(f\"-{whole}.{frac:02d}\") == -(whole * 100 + frac)\n=== END AC amount-decimal-forms ===\n\n=== AC amount-exact-integer ===\nIntent: The result is a plain int and a value too large for exact float representation converts exactly.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\n\ndigits = \"12345678901234567\"\ncents = 99\nresult = amount_cents(f\"{digits}.{cents:02d}\")\nassert type(result) is int\nassert result == int(digits) * 100 + cents\n\nsmall = amount_cents(\"3.07\")\nassert type(small) is int\n=== END AC amount-exact-integer ===\n\n=== AC amount-reject-zero ===\nIntent: Every spelling of zero is rejected with Rejected.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        amount_cents(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nfor text in [\"0\", \"0.0\", \"0.00\", \"-0\", \"-0.0\", \"-0.00\", \"00.00\"]:\n    assert rejected(text), text\n=== END AC amount-reject-zero ===\n\n=== AC amount-reject-malformed ===\nIntent: Malformed amounts are rejected with Rejected rather than another exception type.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        amount_cents(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nbad = [\"1.234\", \"1.000\", \"+1.00\", \"\", \" 1.00\", \"1.00 \", \"1.00\\n\", \".\", \".5\",\n       \"1.\", \"abc\", \"1e3\", \"1,50\", \"--1\", \"1.2.3\", \"1,000.00\"]\nfor text in bad:\n    assert rejected(text), repr(text)\n=== END AC amount-reject-malformed ===\n\n=== AC amount-no-float-source ===\nIntent: The validation module does not use floating point for money.\n\nimport ast\nimport inspect\nimport ledger_app.validation as validation\n\ntree = ast.parse(inspect.getsource(validation))\nnames = {n.id for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n, ast.Name)}\nassert \"float\" not in names\nassert not any(isinstance(n, ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.value, float)\n               for n in ast.walk(tree))\n=== END AC amount-no-float-source ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Do not use floating point for money at any layer.\n- Zero-valued amounts are always rejected, including `0.00` and `-0.00`.\n- A rejected amount writes nothing to standard output.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DateValidation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Date Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Validate real calendar dates written YYYY-MM-DD. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.validation.date |\n| Consumes | cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nTransactions, transfers, and loaded files carry dates. One function decides whether text is an acceptable date, and the stored value is the text exactly as validated.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.validation.date(text: str) -> str`\n\n- Returns the input text unchanged when it is valid.\n- Raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) otherwise.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The text must be exactly a four-digit year, a hyphen, a two-digit month, a hyphen, and a two-digit day, all ASCII digits. No other layout is accepted (`2026-2-3`, `2026/02/03`, `20260203`, surrounding whitespace, and trailing newline are rejected).\n2. The year, month, and day must form a real calendar date. `2026-02-30`, `2027-02-29`, `2026-04-31`, month `00` or `13`, and day `00` are rejected; `2028-02-29` is accepted.\n3. The reported date is not normalized, so storage and output show the same text.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC date-accepts-real-dates ===\nIntent: Real calendar dates, including a leap day, are accepted and returned unchanged.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import date\n\nfor text in [\"2026-01-01\", \"2026-12-31\", \"2026-02-28\", \"2028-02-29\"]:\n    assert date(text) == text\n=== END AC date-accepts-real-dates ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-impossible-dates ===\nIntent: Dates that do not exist on the calendar are rejected with Rejected.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import date\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        date(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nfor text in [\"2026-02-30\", \"2027-02-29\", \"2026-04-31\", \"2026-13-01\",\n             \"2026-00-10\", \"2026-01-00\", \"2026-02-32\"]:\n    assert rejected(text), text\n=== END AC date-rejects-impossible-dates ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-wrong-layout ===\nIntent: Anything other than the YYYY-MM-DD layout is rejected with Rejected.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import date\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        date(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nfor text in [\"2026-2-3\", \"2026/02/03\", \"20260203\", \"\", \"26-02-03\", \"02-03-2026\",\n             \"2026-02-03 \", \" 2026-02-03\", \"2026-02-03\\n\", \"2026-02\", \"abcd-ef-gh\"]:\n    assert rejected(text), repr(text)\n=== END AC date-rejects-wrong-layout ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A rejected date writes nothing to standard output.\n- Dates are never reformatted between input and storage.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DatabaseLocation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Database Location\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Resolve the database location from DATABASE_URL, DATABASE_PATH, or data/app.db. |\n| Depends On | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn |\n| Consumes | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command locates its database the same way. Resolution is a pure function of an explicit environment mapping and the application root, so it is testable without changing the process environment.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn(env, root) -> str`\n\n- `env` is a mapping of environment variable names to values; the function never reads `os.environ` itself.\n- `root` is the application root directory as a string or path.\n- Returns a string DSN for the persistence library.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Order of precedence: `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then the default `data/app.db`.\n2. A variable that is empty counts as unset, so resolution falls through to the next source.\n3. A value beginning `postgres://` or `postgresql://` is a PostgreSQL DSN and is returned unchanged.\n4. Any other value is a SQLite file path. A leading `sqlite://` prefix is stripped first (`sqlite:///tmp/x.db` yields `/tmp/x.db`).\n5. A relative SQLite path, including the default, is resolved against `root` and returned as an absolute path. An absolute path is returned as given.\n6. Resolution never touches the filesystem: it creates no directory and opens nothing.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC location-precedence ===\nIntent: DATABASE_URL wins over DATABASE_PATH, which wins over the default under the root.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom ledger_app.config import resolve_dsn\n\ndef same(a, b):\n    return os.path.realpath(a) == os.path.realpath(b)\n\nroot = tempfile.mkdtemp()\nurl_path = os.path.join(root, \"from_url.db\")\nenv_path = os.path.join(root, \"from_path.db\")\n\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_URL\": url_path, \"DATABASE_PATH\": env_path}, root), url_path)\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_PATH\": env_path}, root), env_path)\nassert same(resolve_dsn({}, root), os.path.join(root, \"data\", \"app.db\"))\n=== END AC location-precedence ===\n\n=== AC location-empty-is-unset ===\nIntent: An empty DATABASE_URL, or an |\n| Description | Open the store through the persistence library and map operational failures to exit 1. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseLocation.md, FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, DATABASE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.store.open_store |\n| Consumes | ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn, persistence.open_database, cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAll commands reach the database through one function. With `prepare=False` the function verifies each declared table can be read and raises when one cannot.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Any persistence error, OS error, or failed connection (unreachable server, unreadable file, a path that is a directory, missing tables) is raised as `cli.errors.OperationalError`, exit 1, one stderr line, no stdout.\n2. Nothing is written to standard output by this function.\n3. Application code imports no database driver (`sqlite3`, `psycopg`); only the generated `persistence` package does.\n4. SQLite is the verified engine; PostgreSQL DSNs are routed to the persistence library unchanged.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-ACCOUNTS-001` — read: probe the accounts table - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`\n- `FEATURE-CATEGORIES-001` — read: probe the categories table - supplies: `category` - returns: `category`\n- `FEATURE-TRANSACTIONS-001` — read: probe the transactions table - supplies: `txn_id` - returns: `txn_id`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC store-opens-usable-database ===\nIntent: open_store with no argument returns a database that sees rows written through the configured store.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledger_app.store import open_store\n\ncode = \"ZZST001\"\nname = \"Store Access\"\nwith db_session() as seeded:\n    seeded.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n    store = open_store()\n    got = store.accounts.get(account=code)\n    assert got is not None\n    value = got[\"name\"] if isinstance(got, dict) else got.name\n    assert value == name\n    assert store.accounts.get(account=\"ZZST002\") is None\n=== END AC store-opens-usable-database ===\n\n=== AC store-unreadable-is-operational ===\nIntent: A Description | Compute account balances and reject any change that would leave one below zero. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-StoreAccess.md, FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, DATABASE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.balances.balance_cents, ledger_app.balances.ensure_non_negative |\n| Consumes | ledger_app.store.open_store, cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAn account's balance is the sum of its transaction amounts in integer cents. No command may leave any account below zero. Every write command computes the net change it would make and asks the guard before its first write.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `ledger_app.balances.balance_cents(db, account: str) -> int` sums the stored amounts of the account's transactions; 0 when it has none.\n- `ledger_app.balances.ensure_non_negative(db, deltas: Mapping[str, int]) -> None` takes the net change in cents per account, adds each to the stored balance, and raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) if any final balance is below zero.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The check is on the final balance a command would leave, not per row: callers combine every row of a command (a whole load file, both transfer legs, an amend's new minus old) into one net delta per account.\n2. A final balance of exactly zero is allowed.\n3. An account with no stored transactions has balance 0; a negative delta on it is rejected, a positive one is accepted.\n4. Every named account is checked before anything is reported; the guard itself writes nothing.\n5. Integer arithmetic only.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-TRANSACTIONS-002` — read: list the transactions of one account - supplies: `account` - returns: `amount`\n\n## Programmatic BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-AmountValidation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Amount Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Parse input amounts into signed integer cents, rejecting zero and malformed values. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.validation.amount_cents |\n| Consumes | cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command that accepts a money amount (`txn post`, `txn amend`, `transfer`, `load transactions`) converts the text to integer cents through one function. Money is never a float at any layer.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.validation.amount_cents(text: str) -> int`\n\n- Returns signed integer cents, a plain `int`.\n- Raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) for any value that is not accepted.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The text must match `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]` over the whole string: an optional minus, one or more ASCII digits, then optionally a period followed by one or two ASCII digits. Use a full match, so a trailing newline is not tolerated.\n2. Conversion uses string splitting and integer arithmetic only. A one-digit fraction is tens of cents (`5.5` is 550 cents); no fraction means whole units.\n3. A value equal to zero (`0`, `0.0`, `0.00`, `-0`, `-0.00`) is rejected.\n4. Rejected shapes include three or more decimals (even `1.000`), a leading `+`, an empty string, surrounding whitespace, a bare `.`, a missing integer part (`.5`), a trailing period (`1.`), thousands separators, exponents, and non-numeric text.\n5. No `float`, `Decimal` of a float, or `round` appears in the implementation.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC amount-decimal-forms ===\nIntent: Two-decimal, one-decimal, whole, and negative inputs convert to the integer cents derived from the supplied parts.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\n\nwhole = 12\nfrac = 50\nassert amount_cents(f\"{whole}.{frac:02d}\") == whole * 100 + frac\n\ntenths = 5\nassert amount_cents(f\"{whole}.{tenths}\") == whole * 100 + tenths * 10\n\nassert amount_cents(str(whole)) == whole * 100\n\nassert amount_cents(f\"-{whole}.{frac:02d}\") == -(whole * 100 + frac)\n=== END AC amount-decimal-forms ===\n\n=== AC amount-exact-integer ===\nIntent: The result is a plain int and a value too large for exact float representation converts exactly.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\n\ndigits = \"12345678901234567\"\ncents = 99\nresult = amount_cents(f\"{digits}.{cents:02d}\")\nassert type(result) is int\nassert result == int(digits) * 100 + cents\n\nsmall = amount_cents(\"3.07\")\nassert type(small) is int\n=== END AC amount-exact-integer ===\n\n=== AC amount-reject-zero ===\nIntent: Every spelling of zero is rejected with Rejected.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        amount_cents(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nfor text in [\"0\", \"0.0\", \"0.00\", \"-0\", \"-0.0\", \"-0.00\", \"00.00\"]:\n    assert rejected(text), text\n=== END AC amount-reject-zero ===\n\n=== AC amount-reject-malformed ===\nIntent: Malformed amounts are rejected with Rejected rather than another exception type.\n\nfrom ledger_app.validation import amount_cents\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\ndef rejected(text):\n    try:\n        amount_cents(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        return True\n    return False\n\nbad = [\"1.234\", \"1.000\", \"+1.00\", \"\", \" 1.00\", \"1.00 \", \"1.00\\n\", \".\", \".5\",\n       \"1.\", \"abc\", \"1e3\", \"1,50\", \"--1\", \"1.2.3\", \"1,000.00\"]\nfor text in bad:\n    assert rejected(text), repr(text)\n=== END AC amount-reject-malformed ===\n\n=== AC amount-no-float-source ===\nIntent: The validation module does not use floating point for money.\n\nimport ast\nimport inspect\nimport ledger_app.validation as validation\n\ntree = ast.parse(inspect.getsource(validation))\nnames = {n.id for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n, ast.Name)}\nassert \"float\" not in names\nassert not any(isinstance(n, ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.value, float)\n               for n in ast.walk(tree))\n=== END AC amount-no-float-source ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Do not use floating point for money at any layer.\n- Zero-valued amounts are always rejected, including `0.00` and `-0.00`.\n- A rejected amount writes nothing to standard output.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DateValidation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Date Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Validate real calendar dates written YYYY-MM-DD. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.validation.date |\n| Consumes | cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nTransactions, transfers, and loaded files carry dates. One function decides whether text is an acceptable date, and the stored value is the text exactly as validated.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.validation.date(text: str) -> str`\n\n- Returns the input text unchanged when it is valid.\n- Raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) otherwise.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The text must be exactly a four-digit year, a hyphen, a two-digit month, a hyphen, and a two-digit day, all ASCII digits. No other layout is accepted (`2026-2-3`, `2026/02/03`, `20260203`, surrounding whitespace, and trailing newline are rejected).\n2. The year, month, and day must form a real calendar date. `2026-02-30`, `2027-02-29`, `2026-04-31`, month `00` or `13`, and day `00` are rejected; `2028-02-29` is accepted.\n3. The reported date is not normalized, so storage and output show the same text.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC input and storage.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DatabaseLocation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Database Location\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Resolve the database location from DATABASE_URL, DATABASE_PATH, or data/app.db. |\n| Depends On | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn |\n| Consumes | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command locates its database the same way. Resolution is a pure function of an explicit environment mapping and the application root, so it is testable without changing the process environment.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn(env, root) -> str`\n\n- `env` is a mapping of environment variable names to values; the function never reads `os.environ` itself.\n- `root` is the application root directory as a string or path.\n- Returns a string DSN for the persistence library.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Order of precedence: `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then the default `data/app.db`.\n2. A variable that is empty counts as unset, so resolution falls through to the next source.\n3. A value beginning `postgres://` or `postgresql://` is a PostgreSQL DSN and is returned unchanged.\n4. Any other value is a SQLite file path. A leading `sqlite://` prefix is stripped first (`sqlite:///tmp/x.db` yields `/tmp/x.db`).\n5. A relative SQLite path, including the default, is resolved against `root` and returned as an absolute path. An absolute path is returned as given.\n6. Resolution never touches the filesystem: it creates no directory and opens nothing.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC location-precedence ===\nIntent: DATABASE_URL wins over DATABASE_PATH, which wins over the default under the root.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom ledger_app.config import resolve_dsn\n\ndef same(a, b):\n    return os.path.realpath(a) == os.path.realpath(b)\n\nroot = tempfile.mkdtemp()\nurl_path = os.path.join(root, \"from_url.db\")\nenv_path = os.path.join(root, \"from_path.db\")\n\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_URL\": url_path, \"DATABASE_PATH\": env_path}, root), url_path)\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_PATH\": env_path}, root), env_path)\nassert same(resolve_dsn({}, root), os.path.join(root, \"data\", \"app.db\"))\n=== END AC location-precedence ===\n\n=== AC location-empty-is-unset ===\nIntent: An empty DATABASE_URL, or an empty DATABASE_PATH, is treated as unset.\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom ledger_app.config import resolve_dsn\n\ndef same(a, b):\n    return os.path.realpath(a) == os.path.realpath(b)\n\nroot = tempfile.mkdtemp()\nenv_path = os.path.join(root, \"from_path.db\")\n\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_URL\": \"\", \"DATABASE_PATH\": env_path}, root), env_path)\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_URL\": \"\", \"DATABASE_PATH\": \"\"}, root),\n            os.path.join(root, \"data\", \"app.db\"))\nassert same(resolve_dsn({\"DATABASE_URL\": \"\"}, root), os.path.join(root, \"data\", \"app.db\"))\n=== END AC location-empty-is-unset ===\n\n=== AC location-sqlite-prefix-and-relative ===\nIntent: The sqlite:// prefix is stripped and relative paths resolve before\nassert not os.path.exists(os.path.join(root, \"data\"))\n=== END AC location-uses-given-env-only ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Never read `os.environ` inside `resolve_dsn`; callers pass the mapping.\n- Resolution performs no filesystem writes; `init` creates the default parent directory.\n- Application code never opens a database driver; the resolved DSN goes to the persistence library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-StoreAccess.md ===\n# FEATURE: Store Access\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Open the store through the persistence library and map operational failures to exit 1. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseLocation.md, FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, DATABASE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.store.open_store |\n| Consumes | ledger_app.config.resolve_dsn, persistence.open_database, cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAll commands reach the database through one function. It resolves the location, opens the store with the shipped persistence library, and turns any operational failure into the one error the CLI maps to exit 1.\n\n## Interface\n\n`ledger_app.store.open_store(dsn: str | None = None, *, prepare: bool = False)`\n\n- With no `dsn`, resolves one using `resolve_dsn(os.environ, application_root)`.\n- Returns the persistence `Database` (`db.accounts`, `db.categories`, `db.transactions`).\n- `prepare=True` is used only by `init`: the declared tables are created idempotently through the persistence library's provisioning. With `prepare=False` the function verifies each declared table can be read and raises when one cannot.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Any persistence error, OS error, or failed connection (unreachable server, unreadable file, a path that is a directory, missing tables) is raised as `cli.errors.OperationalError`, exit 1, one stderr line, no stdout.\n2. Nothing is written to standard output by this function.\n3. Application code imports no database driver (`sqlite3`, `psycopg`); only the generated `persistence` package does.\n4. SQLite is the verified engine; PostgreSQL DSNs are routed to the persistence library unchanged.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-ACCOUNTS-001` — read: probe the accounts table - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`\n- `FEATURE-CATEGORIES-001` — read: probe the categories table - supplies: `category` - returns: `category`\n- `FEATURE-TRANSACTIONS-001` — read: probe the transactions table - supplies: `txn_id` - returns: `txn_id`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC store-opens-usable-database ===\nIntent: open_store with no argument returns a database that sees rows written through the configured store.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledger_app.store import open_store\n\ncode = \"ZZST001\"\nname = \"Store Access\"\nwith db_session() as seeded:\n    seeded.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n    store = open_store()\n    got = store.accounts.get(account=code)\n    assert got is not None\n    value = got[\"name\"] if isinstance(got, dict) else got.name\n    assert value == name\n    assert store.accounts.get(account=\"ZZST002\") is None\n=== END AC store-opens-usable-database ===\n\n=== AC store-unreadable-is-operational ===\nIntent: A location that cannot be opened raises OperationalError and writes nothing to stdout.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nfrom ledger_app.store import open_store\nfrom cli.errors import OperationalError\n\ndirectory = tempfile.mkdtemp()\nout = io.StringIO()\nraised = False\ntry:\n    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out):\n        try:\n            open_store(directory)\n        except OperationalError:\n            raised = True\nfinally:\n    shutil.rmtree(directory, ignore_errors=True)\nassert raised\nassert out.getvalue() == \"\"\n=== END AC store-unreadable-is-operational ===\n\n=== AC store-explicit-dsn-honored ===\nIntent: An explicit DSN is used instead of the configured store, so the two stores are independent.\n\nimport os\nimport          other = open_store(target, prepare=True)\n        except OperationalError:\n            other = None\n        if other is not None:\n            assert other.accounts.get(account=code) is exit 1 on an unusable store.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-BalanceGuard.md ===\n# FEATURE: Balance Guard\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Compute account balances and reject any change that would leave one below zero. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-StoreAccess.md, FEATURE-CliFoundation.md, DATABASE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides | ledger_app.balances.balance_cents, ledger_app.balances.ensure_non_negative |\n| Consumes | ledger_app.store.open_store, cli.errors |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nAn account's balance is the sum of its transaction amounts in integer cents. No command may leave any account below zero. Every write command computes the net change it would make and asks the guard before its first write.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `ledger_app.balances.balance_cents(db, account: str) -> int` sums the stored amounts of the account's transactions; 0 when it has none.\n- `ledger_app.balances.ensure_non_negative(db, deltas: Mapping[str, int]) -> None` takes the net change in cents per account, adds each to the stored balance, and raises `cli.errors.Rejected` (exit 3) if any final balance is below zero.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The check is on the final balance a command would leave, not per row: callers combine every row of a command (a whole load file, both transfer legs, an amend's new minus old) into one net delta per account.\n2. A final balance of exactly zero is allowed.\n3. An account with no stored transactions has balance 0; a negative delta on it is rejected, a positive one is accepted.\n4. Every named account is checked before anything is reported; the guard itself writes nothing.\n5. Integer arithmetic only.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-TRANSACTIONS-002` — read: list the transactions of one account - supplies: `account` - returns: `amount`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC balance-sums-amounts ===\nIntent: balance_cents returns the sum of the account's stored amounts and 0 for an account with none.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledger_app.balances import balance_cents\n\nacct = \"ZZBG001\"\nempty = \"ZZBG002\"\ncat = \"ZZBGC01\"\namounts = [10000, -2550, 300]\nbase = 900001000\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Balance One\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=empty, name=\"Balance Empty\")\n    db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Balance Category\")\n    for i, cents in enumerate(amounts):\n        db.transactions.create(txn_id=base + i, date=\"2026-04-01\", account=acct,\n                               category=cat, amount=cents, memo=\"\")\n    result = balance_cents(db, acct)\n    assert type(result) is int\n    assert result == sum(amounts)\n    assert balance_cents(db, empty) == 0\n=== END AC balance-sums-amounts ===\n\n=== AC db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Balance Category\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=900001100, date=\"2026-04-01\", account=acct,\n                           category=cat, amount=start, memo=\"\")\n    balance = balance_cents(db, acct)\n    ensure_non_negative(db, {acct: -balance})\n    ensure_non_negative(db, {acct: 1})\n    ensure_non_negative(db, {acct: 0})\n    ensure_non_negative(db, {})\n=== END AC guard-allows-zero-and-positive ===\n\n=== AC guard-rejects-below-zero ===\nIntent: A change leaving a balance one cent below zero raises Rejected.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledger_app.balances import balance_cents, ensure_non_negative\nfrom cli.errors import Rejected\n\nacct = \"ZZBG004\"\ncat = \"ZZBGC03\"\nwith db_session()
